# Abilities: the merged Phase 1a list (2026-10-08)

**Merged draft: Phase 1a. Scope decisions applied 2026-10-08 (section 3); independent check pass 1 applied 2026-10-08 with the owner's decisions of that day (`audits/abilities-pass1/applied.md`); 230 abilities (AB-001 to AB-234; AB-126 and AB-127 dropped, AB-201 and AB-203 joined other rows).**

**What this is.** One list of the musical abilities the four Phase 1a drafts found (FABLE.md section 2, phase 1a), merged without losing anything a source supports. Inputs: `docs/classifier/abilities/draft-abrsm-trinity.md` (AT-, 179 rows), `draft-rcm.md` (RC-, 85), `draft-early-levels.md` (EL-, 114), `draft-styles.md` (ST-, 104); the 28 abilities (`#### A…` headings) of `docs/prompts/runs/curriculum-review-2026-10-05/ABILITY-MAP.md`; the 15 tracks of `content/curriculum/00-tracks.json`. One independent pass has been applied (pass 1: `audits/abilities-pass1/rows.md` and `missing.md`, applied as `applied.md`); the next pass has not run. Every row is a research claim resting on its draft rows or, for AB-208 to AB-232, on the source named in the row; pass 1 downloaded and text-searched the citations it names, and the rest of the drafts remain as the drafts left them.

**How the rows were merged (my judgement, listed for the checkers in section 8).**
- Two draft rows are one ability when a learner who can do one can do the other as the sources describe it. Where they differ only in a graded parameter (keys, range, tempo, hands, metre, articulation, length), they are one ability with its parameters by level. Where the reading or sight-reading tables grade an element (a metre, a key, a note value, a mark), the element's row holds both its prepared levels (methods) and its at-sight levels (boards); the general sight-reading row holds the unseen act.
- Disagreements are kept side by side: each member's own 'where they differ' cell is carried verbatim, and a 'Merged:' note states where the merged sources disagree on level, board or method order. Levels are each source's own (ABRSM and Trinity grades, RCM levels, method books and units); none is converted or averaged.
- Scope (the owner, 2026-10-08): abilities away from the keyboard are out. Written theory exercises, other clefs, transposing-instrument writing and writing music out on paper are dropped (section 3). Knowing what is in the music one plays is kept as recognition at the piano (folded rows, and AB-202, AB-204 to AB-207). Clapping, tapping, singing back and said answers to heard music are kept (section 4; formerly BORDERLINE). Practice method done at the piano is kept (AB-208 to AB-215; the owner, 2026-10-08); planning practice away from the keyboard is out.

**Columns.**
- *parameters by level*: my summary of the members' level cells; the cells themselves are in the next column.
- *level range by source* and *sources*: each member's level cell and source cell, carried verbatim with its draft id. Citation keys (`[a27]`, `[tcl]`, `[rcm]`, `[TCL]`, `[LEV]` …) resolve in that draft's section 3; they are scoped to their draft (`[tcl]` in the AT draft is Trinity's classical piano syllabus, `[TCL]` in the ST draft is Trinity Rock & Pop).
- *tracks*: the ST draft's own track cells where an ST row is a member; the rest are assigned here (a reading).
- *prerequisites*: only the EL draft gives them; they are that draft's reading of method order, not a source statement, mapped here to merged ids (`AB-x (EL-y, for EL-z)`: EL-z lists EL-y, which is now in AB-x). Prerequisites inside the same merged row are internal order and are left out.
- *existing ability*: the A-id of the 28 that the row matches fully or in part, or NEW; 'near' names a related A-id that it does not match.
- *BORDERLINE?*: "kept (was BORDERLINE …)" marks the rows section 4 lists; the BORDERLINE category itself is closed (the 2026-10-08 decision, section 3).
- Pipes inside carried cells are replaced by '/'. Quotations of 15 or more words in the draft cells are shortened to their first 12 words and an ellipsis (counted in section 7).
- *Pass 1* notes (2026-10-08) are added inside cells as "Pass 1: …" or "Merged (pass 1 …)"; carried draft text is left verbatim and a pass-1 note supersedes it where they differ. "(curl 2026-10-08, found verbatim)" means the page or PDF was downloaded and the quoted words found in its text by script.

**Abbreviations.** In my text (titles, parameters, 'Merged:' notes): AB = ABRSM (Prep, Init, G1-8), Alfred or Alfred Basic = Alfred's Basic (1A, 1B, Levels), AA = Alfred's Adult, FB = Faber Basic, FA = Faber Adult, HL = Hal Leonard, TCL = Trinity classical, PM = ABRSM Practical Musicianship, Th = ABRSM Theory, RCM L = RCM level, KH = RCM Keyboard Harmony. **In the carried draft cells the drafts' own abbreviations hold, and they collide:** in EL cells `AB` is Alfred's Basic; in AT cells `AB` is ABRSM. The merged ids `AB-001`… are neither.

## 2. Trace: every draft row to its merged row

Every draft ability row (section 1 of each draft) appears here exactly once, checked by the build script (section 7). The EL draft's own removed ids (EL-103, EL-104, EL-106, EL-111; its scope note) are not rows and are not traced.

| RC-020 | Play a **solid** tonic triad in root position and inversions: with rests at L1–4, without rests and hands together at L5–6 | AB-100 |
| RC-021 | Close a chord pattern with the stated cadence progression in its key: I–V–I (L5–6), I–IV–V–I (L7), I–IV–V⁶₄–⁵₃–I (L8), I–vi–IV–V⁶₄–V⁸⁻⁷–I (L9–10). Minor keys use the harmonic minor | AB-105 |
| RC-022 | Play a **broken** tonic four-note chord (root doubled at the octave) in root position and inversions, hands together over 2 octaves | AB-101 |
| RC-023 | Play a **solid** tonic four-note chord in root position and inversions, hands together | AB-100 |
| RC-024 | Play four-note chords in the **broken alternate-note pattern**. At L9 it is an option for tonic chords, the small-hands substitute for solid. At L10 it is required for tonic, dominant 7th and diminished 7th chords | AB-101 |
| RC-025 | Play a **broken** dominant 7th chord of a major key in root position and inversions: each hand alone (1 octave L5, 2 octaves L6), then hands together over 2 octaves (L7–9) | AB-101 |
| RC-026 | Play a **solid** dominant 7th chord in root position and inversions (same hands and ranges as RC-025; L10 too) | AB-100 |
| RC-027 | Play a **broken** leading-tone diminished 7th chord of a minor key in root position and inversions: each hand alone (L6), then hands together (L7–9) | AB-101 |
| RC-028 | Play a **solid** leading-tone diminished 7th chord in root position and inversions | AB-100 |
| RC-029 | Play a tonic arpeggio in root position only, each hand alone, over 2 octaves | AB-102 |
| RC-030 | Play a tonic arpeggio hands together in root position and both inversions: 2 octaves (L7), 4 octaves (L8–10). At L10 the inversions may be played singly or in sequence | AB-102 |
| RC-031 | Play a dominant 7th arpeggio in root position only: each hand alone (L6), then hands together over 2 octaves (L7) and 4 octaves (L8) | AB-103 |
| RC-032 | Play a dominant 7th arpeggio hands together over 4 octaves in root position and all three inversions | AB-103 |
| RC-033 | Play a leading-tone diminished 7th arpeggio in root position only: each hand alone (L6), then hands together (L7–8) | AB-103 |
| RC-034 | Play a leading-tone diminished 7th arpeggio hands together over 4 octaves in root position and inversions | AB-103 |
| RC-035 | Play each technical item at or above the stated minimum metronome speed, in the stated note value (quarters, eighths, triplet eighths, sixteenths) | AB-097 |
| RC-036 | Play every scale, chord and arpeggio pattern from memory, in the key the examiner names. The examiner samples the list | AB-096 |
| RC-037 | Play the patterns ascending and descending, legato unless marked otherwise, with logical fingering, good tone and a steady tempo | AB-098 |
| RC-038 | Perform a programme of contrasting pieces at the level: three (Prep A–L7), four (L8–9), five (L10). The L9 programme is at most 15 minutes and the L10 programme at most 30 | AB-069 |
| RC-039 | Perform a Baroque piece (List A; at L1–2 the list is Baroque and Classical; at L10 it is works by J.S. Bach) | AB-073 |
| RC-040 | Perform a Classical or Classical-style piece. By *reading* of the list contents: sonatina movements at L1–7, sonata movements at L8–10, and complete sonatas offered at L9–10 | AB-074 |
| RC-041 | Perform a Romantic piece. It is its own list at L8–10; at L1–7 it shares a list with 20th- and 21st-century music | AB-075 |
| RC-042 | Perform a post-Romantic, Impressionist, 20th- or 21st-century piece. It is its own list at L8–9; at L10 it splits into List D (post-Romantic, Impressionist, early 20th century) and List E (20th and 21st century) | AB-076 |
| RC-043 | Perform an "Invention" at elementary level (List C). By *reading* of the titles (Bartók *Conversation*, *Follow My Leader*, *Teapot Invention*): a short two-voice piece in which the hands imitate or answer each other | AB-017 |
| RC-044 | Perform a contrapuntal Baroque keyboard work by J.S. Bach, inside List A: two-part inventions (L7–8), three-part sinfonias and a prelude and fugue (L9), preludes and fugues from *The Well-Tempered Clavier* and suite movements (L10) | AB-017 |
| RC-045 | Perform repertoire from memory. Memory marks are optional at Prep A–L8; at L9–10 a mark is deducted for each piece not memorised | AB-067 |
| RC-046 | Follow da capo and dal segno signs in performance; play repeats only where the syllabus says to | AB-044 |
| RC-047 | Perform études at the level: one at L1–2, two technically contrasting ones at L3–10. Memory is not required. The syllabus does not name the skill each étude trains | AB-077 |
| RC-048 | Perform a non-classical arrangement from the Popular Selection List in place of one étude (optional) | AB-078 |
| RC-049 | Clap, tap or sing back the rhythm of a short melody heard twice, after the examiner names the time signature and counts one bar | AB-122 |
| RC-050 | Name a heard triad major or minor. At Prep A–B it follows the first five notes of the scale; at L1 it is played broken then solid; from L2 solid, once | AB-139 |
| RC-051 | Hear a broken triad, then a single note, and name the note as its root, third or fifth | AB-140 |
| RC-052 | Name the quality of a heard chord played solid in close position: major and minor triads and dominant 7th (L5); plus diminished 7th (L6); plus augmented triad (L7–8); major and minor four-note chords in root position and first inversion (L9–10); plus major 7th and minor 7th (L10) | AB-139 |
| RC-053 | Name an interval heard melodically, ascending and descending: m3 and M3 (L1), + P5 (L2), + P4 (L3), + P8 (L4) | AB-135 |
| RC-054 | Name an interval heard melodically then harmonically (L5–9), or in either form (L10). The set grows to all intervals within the octave, including the tritone (L8), and minor and major 9ths (L10) | AB-135 |

| RC-056 | Name a heard three-chord progression (keyboard style, bass rising from the tonic): I–IV–I or I–V–I (L5); minor forms too (L6); plus I–IV–V and i–iv–V (L7) | AB-137 |
| RC-057 | Name each chord of a progression heard twice, with a pause on each chord the second time. Four chords from set lists (L8); four chords from I, IV, V, vi / i, iv, V, VI (L9); five chords plus the cadential ⁶₄ (L10) | AB-137 |
| RC-058 | Play back a single-line melody after the key is named and the tonic chord sounded. The melody uses the first three scale notes (Prep A–B), five notes (L1–4), five notes plus the upper tonic (L5), then the whole scale (L6–8). It is heard twice, or three times from L5, when the learner claps the rhythm or sings the melody after the second hearing | AB-117 |

| RC-060 | Play back a heard diatonic melody and harmonise it with left-hand solid chords from I, IV and V | AB-120 |

| RC-062 | The same, making an eight-bar **contrasting** period; minor keys and an upbeat are possible at L8 | AB-147 |
| RC-063 | Play back a two-bar opening, complete the question (antecedent) phrase, and improvise an answer (consequent) to make an eight-bar contrasting period | AB-147 |
| RC-064 | Tap a steady beat for one bar, then keep tapping while speaking, tapping or clapping a written rhythm in **simple** time (2/4, 3/4, 4/4), with metric accent. From L4 the rhythm is that of a given melody | AB-123 |
| RC-065 | Do the same in **compound** time (6/8) | AB-123 |
| RC-066 | Sight-play two four-note melodies: one in the treble with the right hand alone, one in the bass with the left hand alone. They move by step in one direction (a note may repeat), with fingering only on the first note | AB-046 |
| RC-067 | Sight-play a short melody divided between the hands, one hand at a time, in five-finger positions. At L1 it is four bars, in C, G or F major or A minor, with fingering on the first note of each hand | AB-046 |
| RC-068 | Sight-play a melody divided between the hands that moves **beyond the five-finger position** | AB-046 |
| RC-069 | Sight-play a four-bar passage **hands together** (L3 in 4/4; L4 in 3/4 or 4/4) | AB-046 |
| RC-070 | Sight-play a passage comparable to repertoire three levels lower (L5 reads Level 2 pieces … L10 reads Level 7 pieces). Length grows from 8 to 16 bars and keys reach up to five sharps or flats | AB-046 |
| RC-071 | Sight-play in compound time (6/8) | AB-028 |
| RC-072 | Sight-play a passage that begins with an upbeat (anacrusis) | AB-036 |
| RC-073 | Sight-play in any time signature | AB-029 |

| RC-079 | Improvise the missing upper or lower part of a short two-part contrapuntal piece in half and quarter notes, using varied contrapuntal motion | AB-115 |

| RC-082 | Harmonise a given soprano and bass in keyboard style: from some functional symbols or figures (KH9), from figured bass (KH10) | AB-112 |
| RC-083 | Make up an accompaniment in a fitting style for a given melody, with or without chord symbols | AB-109 |

## 3. Dropped (scope and the owner's rulings)

**Decided 2026-10-08 (the orchestrator, on the owner's 2026-10-08 instruction to use common sense for what a learner needs to progress):** knowledge that serves playing is in, as recognition in the music one plays (rows folded into existing abilities, and AB-201 to AB-207); clapping, tapping and singing back are in as ordinary abilities, answered at the piano too; making up one's own piece is in and keeping it by recording counts (folded into AB-154). Still out: alto and tenor clefs (AT-158), writing for transposing instruments (AT-175), and writing a composition out on paper.

**Pass 1 (2026-10-08).** The owner dropped AB-126 (singing from a score at sight) and AB-127 (singing an interval): they are not singing back, and a learner does not need them to play (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-08, "Those sound good, k"). AT-023 and ST-165 (writing one's composition down), which the reinstatement had folded into AB-154, are dropped: writing music out on paper is out by FABLE section 2 and by the decision above; AB-154 keeps the piece made and performed.

## 6. Open research items (not gaps: a gap means what code cannot verify, FABLE phase 3)

Renamed in pass 1 (2026-10-08, on the owner's word): these were GAP-01 to GAP-07; the pass-1 audit files (`rows.md`, `missing.md`) keep that wording. None of these items is an ability in section 1. Each item's status after pass 1 is in bold. All 25 rows proposed in pass 1 were found in their sources (AB-208 to AB-232), so none is listed here as unsourced; the unsourced candidates are the checker's own (OPEN-08 to OPEN-11).

- **OPEN-01.** Practice method at the keyboard. **Closed in pass 1:** AB-208 to AB-215, in scope by the owner's decision (2026-10-08). Planning practice away from the keyboard (interleaving, plateaus, how long to practise) stays out; "stop when it hurts" is AB-004's member EL-113.
- **OPEN-02.** Ensemble functions. **Closed in pass 1:** counting in AB-216; re-entering when lost and following head and ending cues AB-217; trading fours AB-153 with AB-217; playing in guitar keys AB-039 and AB-116 (no separate row; no guitar-specific source read).
- **OPEN-03.** Physical and rhythmic demands. **Closed in part in pass 1:** leaps AB-218, two against three AB-219, pedal depth (half and flutter) AB-220, sostenuto AB-221, trills AB-222, tremolo AB-223, glissando AB-224. Repeated-note technique is AB-018 (Trinity Rock & Pop's "faster repeated notes", syllabus PDF p.39, Grade 3, is a speed parameter of it); rotation is carried in AB-222 and AB-223. **Still open:** three against four (OPEN-09) and hemiola (OPEN-10).
- **OPEN-04.** Reading a piano-vocal-guitar song score. **Closed in pass 1:** AB-230.
- **OPEN-05.** Carols for people singing. **Covered** (pass 1 verdict): AB-193 (hymn in parts), AB-194 (introduction giving key and tempo), AB-195 (written accompaniment for people singing) and AB-232 (choosing a key for the voices). No carol-specific source read.
- **OPEN-06.** Sources not read. **Open in part.** Read in pass 1: Trinity Rock & Pop Keyboards grade pages 2, 4 and 7 and its syllabus PDF (AB-218, AB-219, AB-223, AB-224). Of the style draft's 30 uncovered lines, pass 1 found the classical technique and reading lines covered by existing rows, programme times and exam formats not abilities, history and other instruments' parts out, "pedalling, hand stretch, page-turns" given AB-229 (hand stretch is AB-011 and AB-026), and Mauleón's "Song Form and Structures" a parameter of form. **Still unread:** Levine ch.18 "Four-Note Scales" and ch.22 "Loose Ends"; LCM's Jazz Piano Handbook exercise; the 14 unread Hal Leonard style titles; ABRSM and Trinity alternative tests; ABRSM Performance Grades one-hand options; Trinity Piano Plus 2 extracts and repertoire list; Faber Level 1-3B internal order; Alfred Ear Training 1A. ## 8. Calls made in merging, for the checkers and the owner

### 8.1 Merge calls, with the pass-1 verdicts and the owner's decisions

Each call as the merger made it, then the two pass-1 verdicts (`rows.md` section 2, `missing.md` section 2.2) and what now stands.

- AB-083 holds the three minor forms in one row; the boards offer the forms as the candidate's choice until Grade 6, which reads as a parameter. **Pass 1:** both agree. Stands.
- AB-082 holds scales over one to four octaves, hands separately and together, legato and staccato (hands, range and articulation as parameters); contrary-motion, chromatic, thirds-and-sixths-apart, double-note and octave scales stay separate rows. **Pass 1:** both agree; missing.md's condition (hands together stays a named parameter with its own levels) holds: the parameters cell names hands separately, then together, by level. Stands.
- Chords: block (AB-100) and broken (AB-101) rows carry every chord type as a parameter; arpeggios split into triad (AB-102) and seventh/ninth (AB-103). **Pass 1:** both agree; rows.md adds that AB-203 duplicates AB-100 and AB-105: joined AB-105 (the brief's instruction; a learner who plays I-IV-V7 in a key can name the notes of its I, IV and V triads). Stands.
- AB-046 folds hands and position into the one sight-reading row. **Pass 1:** both agree; missing.md adds that the methods inside sight-reading are separate abilities: AB-227 (reading ahead) and AB-228 (leaving notes out) added. Stands.
- AB-023 merges treble and bass clef reading with the reading range; AB-024 keeps direction and interval reading apart. **Pass 1:** both agree; AB-201 (naming the notes of a score) duplicated AB-023 and joined it. Stands.
- Metre is split four ways (AB-027 to AB-030). **Pass 1:** both agree. Stands.
- AB-147 merged call-and-response over a groove with RCM's period answers and Keyboard Harmony's consequents with a bass. **Pass 1:** rows.md agree; missing.md split. **The owner, 2026-10-08: split.** AB-147 keeps the beginner call-and-response and the RCM period answers; AB-233 holds the Keyboard Harmony consequents with a bass (KH9-10). AB-148 stays apart.
- AB-141 (jazz chords, progressions and modes by ear) kept apart from AB-139 (chord quality by ear). **Pass 1:** rows.md disagree in part; missing.md agree. **The owner, 2026-10-08:** the seventh-chord qualities (major 7, minor 7, dominant 7) moved into AB-139; AB-141 keeps function, substitution and mode.
- AB-154 merges the Trinity composition craft rows into one compose-and-perform row. **Pass 1:** both agree; its writing-down citations (AT-023, ST-165) removed and dropped (section 3). Stands.
- AB-131 merges dynamics, articulation and tempo by ear; AB-133 and AB-134 apart. **Pass 1:** both agree; AB-134's "key and modulations" clause removed. Stands.
- AB-195 merged a written accompaniment with a soloist and chord-based accompaniment of singers. **Pass 1:** rows.md agree conditionally; missing.md split. **The owner, 2026-10-08: split.** AB-195 is the written accompaniment (with a soloist, and with people singing from the hymn book: ST-123 placed here, the merger's reading); AB-234 is accompanying a singer from chord symbols. Citations fixed: ILPN-227 to AB-197; the Berklee Latin search-result quote removed.
- AB-062 keeps AT-004 (realise the printed detail of a piece) as its own row although it aggregates the 1.D element rows. **Pass 1:** both agree. Stands.
- Pass 1 left apart the pairs missing.md named as possible merges (AB-210 with AB-211, AB-227 with AB-228, AB-222 with AB-223): a learner can do one of each pair and not the other.

- Composition is kept as making and performing one's own piece (AB-154); the writing-down rows (AT-023, ST-165) are dropped (pass 1; FABLE section 2).
- AB-068 (learn and memorise a tune by labelling its progressions) is kept because its outcome is playing from memory.
- AB-197 (sing while playing) is kept: the learner plays.
- Practice method at the piano is in (the owner, 2026-10-08): AB-208 to AB-215. Planning practice away from the keyboard stays out.
- AB-126 (singing from a score at sight) and AB-127 (singing an interval) are dropped (the owner, 2026-10-08).
- AB-112 (figured bass and keyboard-style progressions) is kept, at the advanced levels only (the owner, 2026-10-08).
- A5.1 now rests on in-scope recognition rows (AB-204 and AB-205, with AB-068 and AB-137; AB-206 is near it); A6.2's writing-down part stays out (section 5).
